I get these stretches where I feel really bright, like strong, like I can handle everything on my own. But then if my husband or a client questions me about something, I snap at them a lot harder than I mean to.\n\nDoctor: When you feel especially bright and strong, do you need much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes. A lot of nights I only sleep maybe two or three hours, and then I’m up like I’m ready to go. I’ll start scrubbing floors or sorting papers, and I don’t feel tired the way I normally would after so little sleep.\n\nDoctor: Do these concerns about people watching you happen only when you’re feeling especially bright and strong?\n\nPatient: No, not only then. There was a quieter time for a few weeks where I was sleeping more and not running around as much, but I still heard that voice by the kitchen window. And I still believed the agency was watching us.\n\nDoctor: For at least a month, have you been hearing that voice or believing the agency is watching you?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s been much longer than a month. The voice and that feeling like people are watching us… it’s been going on for about 11 months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least 2 weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I’ve had low days, but I can’t say for sure it was most of the day nearly every day for two weeks.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a full month when you were hearing the voice or felt watched, but you were not feeling especially bright or strong?\n\nPatient: Yes. There was a quieter stretch for a few weeks where I wasn’t feeling all bright or strong like that. I was sleeping more and not rushing around so much, but I still heard the voice by the kitchen window, and I still felt like the agency was watching us.\n\nDoctor: Before these experiences began, had you been diagnosed with a neurological condition like epilepsy, a brain injury, or another brain illness?\n\nPatient: Not that I know of. I haven’t been told I had epilepsy or a brain injury or anything like that before this started.\n\nDoctor: During those bright, strong periods, have you felt unusually grand or like you had special abilities or importance?\n\nPatient: Yes. During those times I felt like I was the only one who really had the sense and the strength to protect my husband and my home. Sometimes it felt bigger than that, like even people at church needed me to keep them safe from what was coming.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least 2 weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I’ve had low days, but I don’t know that it was most of the day nearly every day for two whole weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you felt depressed nearly every day for at least 2 weeks?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I’ve felt down at times, but I can’t say for sure it was nearly every day for two weeks straight.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that other people could put thoughts into your mind or take thoughts out of it?\n\nPatient: Not really like putting thoughts in or taking them out. It’s more that I know people are watching me. The hospital people and some folks from the agency have been watching our house and my cleaning routes, checking whether I’m taking proper care of my husband.\n\nDoctor: During those bright, strong periods, did you find yourself talking much more than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t really think of myself as talking a lot more than usual during those times. Maybe I was more direct or had more to say, but I haven’t really noticed it as a big change.\n\nDoctor: During those bright, strong periods, were you much more active than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes, I guess I am. I’ll get up before it’s even light out and start cleaning, cooking, making phone calls about appointments, writing new lists. I’ll take on errands for church too, even when people are telling me I need to slow down.\n\nDoctor: During those bright, strong periods, were you much more active than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes, like I said, I do get much more active. I’ll be up before daylight getting started—cleaning, cooking, calling about appointments, making lists. And I’ll take on things for church too, even when people are telling me I need to slow down.\n\nDoctor: During those bright, strong periods, did other people tell you that you were talking much more than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t really remember anyone saying that to me. They might tell me I’m doing too much or moving too fast, but I don’t recall them saying I was talking a lot more.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least 2 weeks when you were hearing the voice or felt watched, while you were not feeling especially bright or strong?\n\nPatient: Yes. There was a quieter time that lasted a few weeks, when I wasn’t feeling so strong or rushing around. I was sleeping more, taking things slower, but I still heard the voice by the kitchen window, and I still felt like the agency was watching us.\n\nDoctor: Before the voice and the feeling of being watched began, were you diagnosed with dementia?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, nobody told me I had dementia before all that started, not that I remember.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been told by a doctor that you have a brain disease or another medical condition affecting your brain?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so.
